id=\"offerings_update_form_93476_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eI have recently realized that I did some updates on our 2nd campaign but have not done anything on this one. I am very sorry about that. It has been a turbulent several years. By the time we finally got our facility put together we went from not being able to fill orders to the freight market crash and recession. The order book evaporated. More recently the trade wars made everything much more expsensive to both buy and sell. 20% of our orders were to Canada and all of those dissappeared.\u0026nbsp;\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eWe are still making and selling QuickLoadz, but starting in 2024 we partnered with Horizon Motor to build EV trucks. Horizon Motor did a nationwide search and found QuickLoadz. We were a perfect fit in that we have expertise in EV, we build our own batteries for our trailers, heavy manufacturing, and lots of skilled people with lots of fancy equipment. The work was very sucessful. Other EV truck manfacturers burned through 100's of millions to billions of dollars and never delivered and often never got anything actually built. QuickLoadz set a record from start to EPA, CARB, HVIP approvals then building trucks and now delivering trucks. The model was extremely low capital, entirely relying on QuickLoadz existing infrastructure to pull it off. There are a lot of orders, there are trucks getting finished and delivered.\u0026nbsp;\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eIt is very Geofencing, telematics NHTSA, dealership rules, it has been much, much more than just physically putting the first ones in the US together.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eAs a quick summary of the Echassis, hundreds of thousands of refrigerated containers come and go from the ports. Right now everytime one of them is moved it requires a diesel generator. We are providing a battery Mobile Power Unit that is integrated into the container chassis, provide clean power to the refrigerated container and help pull the load, so the pulling truck can get 30 to 40% better fuel milage. We are taking it to the IANA Intermodal Expo. Clock is ticking to get it done. id=\"offerings_update_form_38992_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eWhy QuickLoadz hard shift to electric?\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe average semi gets 6.5 miles/gallon, average diesel cost is $4/gallon. That gives a $0.62 per mile cost.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe average electric semi uses 1.9 kwh/mile, Commercial power is $0.13/kw. That gives $0.07 per mile cost.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThat is everyday savings, everytime the trucker fills up. Fuel is a truckers biggest expense. If that trucker can pay $0.07 per mile instead of $0.62 per mile they suddenly are making money. Plus the much lower cost of maintenance on an electric vs diesel. So if you don't care about the huge emissions saving and you are not worried about climate change. Then just plain economics means it makes a lot of sense. Freight rates go down, truckers make more money, the enviroment gets less polluted. All around a win. Gotta love that.\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-marginTop1_5 s-marginPullHoriz1 s-paddingHoriz1 s-borderTop1 u-displayFlex u-alignItemsCenter Only 1% of heavy truck production is expected to be Electric in 2024. Why? Electric semi trucks are expensive, and they have very limited range.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"text-align: center;\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eQuickLoadz has solved this problem\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eQuickLoadz has worked on designing and testing an Eaxle system for years. In late 2023 QuickLoadz partnered with Horizon Motor to bring their Eaxle system to market. Initial response has led us to pivot/expand this sub-product into a product that can immediately be installed on any trailer. This turns any combination vehicle into a plug-in hybrid. 30% better fuel econ makes this ROI work for ICE trucks. This enormous range extension makes electric trucks work. At the TMC show we rolled out the Eaxle, At the ACT show in Las Vegas we will bring a completed container chassis with this system installed.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eStep 1, replace the rear axle on container chassis. Turn that container chassis into a plug in electric hybrid with 30% fuel savings. Plus..... add an invertor and plug in refrigerated sea shipping containers, eliminate gen set emissions and collect some hefty incentives.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eStep 2, replace the rear axle on refrigerated dry freight vans, get 30% fuel savings, much longer life than the diesel powered coolers currently on refrigerated dry freight vans.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eStep 3, replace the rear axle on any heavy truck. In a day you could turn any heavy truck into a plug in electric hybrid with 30% fuel savings. All of the advantages of diesel, long range, quick fill up and all of the advantages of the plug in hybrid.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"text-align: center;\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSize of the market\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp style=\"text-align: left;\"\u003eThere are 2 million ICE heavy trucks just in the US. there are 5.6 million semi trailers. id=\"offerings_update_form_31667_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eEPOWER!! We used to only offer the 38HP EFI Kohler gasoline engine to power our trailers. In 2023 we started offering the all electric. Instantly 90% of the orders went all electric. Makes sense, always ready to go, always charged up, no engine to check on oil or gasoline. But it did create a scramble to build battery packs fast enough. Step 1) charge each cell to the top. Step 2) link all of the cells together and let them cross balance. Each step takes about a day, so I think we are going to need more battery assembly benches.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eDon't forget to check out the new CF campaign that just went live on application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.presentationml.presentation\" aria-required=\"true\" placeholder=\"Description\" name=\"offerings_update[body]\" id=\"offerings_update_form_17877_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eGood News and Bad News:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI will start with the Bad News:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhen I first started planning on scaling production I found a Robotic Welding company VC backed with big, big money (that shall remain nameless for legal reasons and here after be called Roby) that met with us many many times and was convinced that they could build a large scale robotic welder that would completely weld together our trailers. We would tack them together, then presto, in less than a day we would have a robotically welded together assembly then a full trailer. Many, many meetings with our engineers and their engineers. A contract was signed, it was great because it had a low monthly cost and would allow the entire production to scale. Our production scaling was based around this process. The robot was late, became even later, meanwhile we are getting further behind. I didn't want to invest a lot of money in traditional manufacturing knowing this robot was coming. I won't go into all of the dirty details, but eventually when pressed they admitted the robot just couldn't do it.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThis means that our entire production was vastly slowed, and 2022 wasn't any better than 2021. Other delays happened like the new laser wasn't making parts until April, We are perpetually late on delivering because of this and generally do our best to catch up. The backlog still stood at around $2M in April.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eSummary on the bad news: I blundered in believing in Roby, putting us a year back and irritating customers.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eGood News:\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e1) The backlog is now around $1.5M with new orders still coming in despite our dropping down to 1 salesperson. In addition a hard shift has occurred to move back to non-Roby based manufacturing with 6 new welders hired, more people in fabrication, more staff in engineering. More welding machines. The laser is cranking away making parts. The new press is cranking away bending parts, the new crane is scheduled to arrive in August (8 months late). The main fabrication floor is getting redesigned away from the Roby model and into a more traditional manufacturing model. We look likely to get out enough units to drop our backlog from 200 days to 90 days and then some more drop. We are looking to bring in another salesperson.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e2) Phillip Reynolds has joined the team as our new CFO. Phillip was brought in by the Board of Directors of our neighbor startup in the industrial park and in 4 years was instrumental in that company selling for $230M.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e3) We are going to do another Republic crowd funding raise.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eSummary Good News: All of that equipment we bought is finally arriving, starting to make parts and fabrication is currently 2x and accelerating. Sales are still up. We have started putting together the team to build the company up for a 4 or 5 year acquisition.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eOverall summary:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI appologize for the giant blunder. I really believed that the company Roby could build the robot, I met with them every week, visited their plant numerous times, I think that they thought that they could do it. But still there were clues. Like when we would send assemblies up to their plant to be welded we were under the impression that they were testing the robot. When we got them back it was clear they weren't robotically welded and instead Roby had hired a large crew to hand weld things together. At that point we really started pressing them which finally led to the admission that they couldn't do it. I probably should have pressed harder and sooner.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eNow that we have shifted back to where we should have been a year ago things are really starting to accelerate.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-marginTop1_5 s-marginPullHoriz1 s-paddingHoriz1 s-borderTop1 u-displayFlex id=\"offerings_update_form_16186_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThis is kinda neat, of the tens of thousands of applications for Amazon Prime Business Grants QuickLoadz was choosen as one of the top 15. If we win we get $25,000 but really the best part is hopefully someone at Amazon will notice that we can change the way freight moves. You can only vote if you are an Amazon Prime Business customer so I don't know that very many of you can directly help vote but it is nice that Amazon thought that QuickLoadz is worthy of being in the top 15.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eFrom Amazon Business Grant info:\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003e3. How can I vote?\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eYou must log into your Amazon Business account in order to vote. After logging in, the vote form will appear on amazon.com/smallbusinessgrants above the \"Frequently asked questions\" section. The voting form will not be visible if you are not logged in or do not have an Amazon Business account. Please be aware that if you are a new Amazon Business customer, it could take up to 48 hours for the voting form to display.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003e4. s-marginBottom1 s-marginLeft0_5 u-hidden'\u003e\nEntry must be under 140 characters.\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='offerings-updates-shared-form__body-wrapper'\u003e\n\u003cdiv application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.presentationml.presentation\" aria-required=\"true\" placeholder=\"Description\" name=\"offerings_update[body]\" id=\"offerings_update_form_13620_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThe new laser is finally working and making parts. This is a big step forward and will greating improve the fabrication sides' speed. We still have issues of not being able to get the steel we want in the shapes we want. In short we want high tensile steel in the particular shape of our I beams. Unfortunately the mills are still just making the most common shapes in the most common steels and unless we can order 200 tons of a particular shape in a particular strength we aren't getting it. But our engineering depart has devised a work around that will use flat steel which we can get in most tensile strengths.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eI mentioned the laser is finally up and running as of last week, this was a 6 month project, but other key items we are still working around such as our overhead crane replacement and misc other production tools. are still months out with no clear deadline for when we get them. The giant plasma table is working partially, it is also waiting on parts to be fully functional, but then since we can't get the 40' lengths of steel is is designed to cut it doesn't really matter.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe switch to the all electric battery system is almost universal. Only 1 unit recently is shipping with the gasoline engine. Everything else is shipping electric. Engineering is stil not quite happy with the battery packs and we are tinkering with different versions including just buying prismatic LiFePo4 cells and making our own. This is probably where we are headed at this point.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eIn order to accelerate and hire in more personell, we are considering doing another CF raise. I am leaning toward StartEngine because it exposes us to another set of investors. Republic has been great to deal with and we have seen some of our investors be excellent in providing introductions, we did lose a fair number of investors from CA and NY because Republic had a glitch right when we were trying to raise and those investors could not come in. The glitch is long since fixed, but I like the idea of having an entirely new batch of CF investors helping QuickLoadz along. I'll keep you informed and send out an email if we move forward with another CF round.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eStill it is all looking good, production is getting faster, we have pretty close to $2M in back orders with just 1 salesperson. We continue to get multiple orders. Engineering and I are very excited about seeing all of the electric units heading out. Produciton is a little less excited because it means there are a lot of changes. Almost from build to build we tweek the electric models. Changes mean slower production and confusion, but it is starting to settle out.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eSean\u003c/p\u003e\u003c/textarea\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class='s-marginTop1_5 s-marginPullHoriz1 s-paddingHoriz1 s-borderTop1 id=\"offerings_update_form_10446_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eA freight startup which was not doing well pivoted to providing a dedicated news outlet for logistics and freight. I was of the \"That will never work\" opinion. It was a long struggle but FreightWaves has emerged into that role and is the goto for people in the industry. Super impressive what the founder Craig Fuller did. Freightwaves has done a couple of more pivots and most of their revenue comes from subscriptions to the massive amount of data that they collect in real time, it is called \"Sonar\". Once again, really impressive.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eTheir most popular Podcast started out as a joky show called What The Truck?!? Which although entertaining, was mainly a complaint fest from truck drivers. It has evolved into a really interesting show that focuses on various startups and what are the trends in freight. Tim Dooner the producer keeps it interesting while \u0026nbsp;doing a great job of finding different ways to see what is going on in the logistics industry. Tim interviews everyone from pallet manufacturers, who see the trends in advance to autonomous truck to Amazon store owners. Really interesting variety of information.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eQuickLoadz appeared on the show for a 3rd time yesterday for a live demo during FreightWaves Global Supply Chain Week 2023. FreightWaves does a lot of conferences, really impressive how well run they are. QuickLoadz did one in Bentonville AR where I loaded a sea shipping container live at our plant in Athens from the stage in Bentonville.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eYesterday's appearance was a nice bit of free promo. id=\"offerings_update_form_8488_offerings_update_body\"\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eIT LIVES!!! A year ago we realized that we couldn't get the custom beams from our vendors we needed and so the search for a 40' plasma table began. 6 months ago I found one, months of putting together 3 semi loads of parts. Craig from QuickLoadz was fantastic in figuring this thing out. Today Rick from Cutting Solutions got the table to do its first cuts. There is another bundle just on the other side out of frame.\u0026nbsp;\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eWhy do we need a 40' x 12' 6 head plasma cutting machine? Well, we have been waiting on cut I-beams from our suppliers for 3 months. Even that was a compromise, we couldn't get the steel we wanted, we couldn't get the shapes we wanted, etc. With this machine we will be able to custom cut and manufacture our beams.
